# Skills · A · part 174

846 entries, answer-first to anthropic-frontend-design. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **answer-first**: How to reply and how to write documents. Conclusion in the first sentence, length proportional to the question, no process narration, no revision history inside deliverables, no AI filler. Load this for every reply longer than a few lines and before writing any .md/.html/.txt document, report, post, plan or prototype copy. Also load when a task involves bulk edits or an instruction that could be read two ways. Enforced by a Stop hook (lints the reply, forces one rewrite) and a PostToolUse hook (lints written prose files). ([ChufanS008/answer-first](https://github.com/ChufanS008/answer-first/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md))
- **answer-first**: Shape responses for scanning: verdict on line 1, evidence as file:line, tables for 3+ comparable items, capped list lengths, no preamble or recap, and an explicit list of what was skipped or left unverified. ALWAYS use this skill when the user says "be terse", "be brief", "too verbose", "shorter answers", "just the answer", "stop explaining", "cut the preamble", "tl;dr", "answer first", "make it scannable", or complains that a reply buried the point. Stays in force for the rest of the session until the user says "normal mode". Governs the assistant's own prose only — never code, file contents… ([nsollazzo/ship-kit](https://github.com/nsollazzo/ship-kit/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/answer-first) · 2 copies)

- **answer-first-structure**: Decide the bottom line before writing and put it in the first sentence, with only decision-relevant support after it. - **answer-from-corpus**: Source-grounded answer to a question via shape-aware retrieval. Classifies the query as a named lookup, a diagnostic, or a synthesis, then runs the right protocol. Stops on sub-claim coverage, not on counts. - **antheo-explain**: Explains how an interface was built, from a live page, a screenshot, a video or code. Use for "how did they do this?", "what makes this feel so good?", "how is this effect built?", "explain this animation", or before recreating something you admire. ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-explain))
- **antheo-layout**: Lays out screens and pages so they read clearly and hold up at every width, from a 320px phone to a 2560px monitor, in long languages and right to left, through the one job, hierarchy, grouping by space, one content edge, widths, and responsive structure. Use when laying out a screen, or when something breaks on a phone, looks empty on a big screen, or feels cluttered. ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-layout))
- **antheo-mobile**: Makes a web app feel native on phones and touch screens. It starts from the symptom (the page zooms into a field, hover sticks after a tap, content hides under the notch or the keyboard, the page bounces, taps lag, the bottom bar jumps) and fixes the cause, then sets the phone shell, stacked actions, bottom sheets and tables that scroll sideways. Use for "this feels wrong on my phone", "make it work on mobile", "feels like a website, not an app", or any change that will be used on touch. ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-mobile))
- **antheo-moment**: Builds a component's one signature moment, the animation people remember, and only on a rare, meaningful event (a code accepted, a payment sent, a file landed, a list finished), never on things used all day. Use when a component should feel special at its peak, or when asked for "delight". ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-moment))
- **antheo-motion**: Decides whether a thing should move, then gives it exact timing, easing and distance (presses, hovers, menus, dialogs, sheets, drags, page transitions) with reduced motion handled. Use when UI feels janky, static or overdone, when a drawer or drag feels wrong, or before shipping any animation. ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-motion))
- **antheo-motion-audit**: Audits the motion across a whole app or folder, not one animation. It inventories every transition and animation with a scanner, then works in one of four modes. Find shows where motion would help and what must stay still, read-only. Remove cuts motion that shouldn't exist. Review is a strict pass that ends in Approve or Block. Plan writes ranked, self-contained fix plans another agent can carry out. Use for "audit our animations", "the app feels sluggish", "too much motion", "where should things animate", "review the motion before release", or "tokenise our transitions". ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-motion-audit))
- **antheo-review**: Checks a running screen, page, component or change in a real browser at seven widths in light and dark and answers "ready to ship?" first, with every finding measured, located and given its cheapest fix. Use for "check this before I ship", "review my page", "what broke in this change", or after building any UI. ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-review))
- **antheo-stress**: Breaks a component or screen on purpose before anyone else does. It renders every state on one page and pushes real, awkward content through every slot (long, empty, ten times the realistic count, right to left, zoomed, slow). Then it records what held and what broke. Use before shipping a component, for "test the edge cases", "what if the name is long", empty, loading and error states, or mock data. ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-stress))
- **antheo-surface**: Gives every surface its depth from what it sits on, whether it rises or sinks, its fill, edge, drop and corner, in light and dark, plus hairlines, translucent chrome, image outlines, optical alignment and icon sizing. Use when UI looks flat, boxy, muddy in dark mode or "cheap", when cards and menus need depth, or when shadows, borders, radii or icons look off. ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-surface))
- **antheo-system**: Works with an app that already exists. It extracts the design system the app really uses, maps every hard-coded value to a token by role, migrates screen by screen without visible change, documents components from their code, and keeps a record of the person's taste so the next agent starts where the last one stopped. Use for "make our UI consistent", "we hardcode colours everywhere", "extract tokens", "do we actually use our tokens?" (a report that changes nothing), "migrate to the new components", "document this component", "remember that I prefer…", or before building into an existing code… ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-system))
- **antheo-tokens**: Generates a product's design tokens from a short brief (colour, type, space, corners, shadows and motion, for light and dark, every contrast pair checked) and names them so every screen reuses them. Use when starting a project, adding dark mode, or when a codebase types raw colours and sizes. ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-tokens))
- **antheo-type**: Sets how text looks and reads, from the faces to a scale with few sizes, weights by size, line height and tracking by size, line length, wrapping, numbers and loading fonts without a jump. Use for type scales, choosing fonts, text that feels cramped, loud or hard to read, or text that wraps badly. ([antheo-design/skills](https://github.com/antheo-design/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/antheo-type))
- **antheo-writing**: Writes and fixes every word in an interface (buttons, labels, errors, empty states, confirmations, toasts, onboarding) so it is plain, specific and ready to translate, with numbers, dates and money formatted for each language. - **anthias-release**: Release, fleet, CI and observability operations for Anthias — balena cloud API + balenaOS track topology, CalVer version stamping and the release-cut/OTA sequencing, the Docker image builder + toolchain, CI-runs-on-merge-SHA behavior, host test isolation, Sentry conventions/diagnostics, and telemetry.
